  "orchestrator_guidance": "First iteration; follow gate recommendation: ITERATE. Verify unresolved flags against the plan and project code before accepting.",
  "robustness": "standard",
  "signals": {
    "iteration": 1,
    "idea": "# Brief: stop the cross-boundary contract bleeding\n\n[all-codex/standard +prep]\n\n## Background and goal\n\nReigh is a production AI/ML task system split across three independently deployed surfaces speaking an implicit wire-level protocol with no contract definition, no codegen, no version negotiation. In one debugging session today the team hit three different flavors of the same class of bug \u2014 each one an *asymmetric contract evolution* that produced silent runtime failures. This is the fourth documented recurrence in five months; the same engineer has now introduced and silently broken the same `ready_for_tasks` contract twice, four months apart.\n\nGoal of this sprint: ship a coherent set of guardrails that make this class of bug **structurally loud** (and in two specific places, structurally impossible). Not perfect prevention. Bleeding stopped, recurrence cost capped, future change shapes safer.\n\nThe fix is being shipped at runtime via the existing Postgres surface plus a small operational sentinel \u2014 explicitly NOT via a build-time codegen contracts package. That package may be the right end state in 6+ months; it is the wrong first move for one solo engineer in feature-shipping mode. Stop the bleeding first; centralize at the layer that already has a chokepoint; defer the perfect-protocol design.\n\n## System under change\n\nThree repos in `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh-workspace/`:\n\n- **`reigh-app/`** \u2014 TypeScript. Supabase edge functions, DB migrations, frontend.\n- **`reigh-worker/`** \u2014 Python. Runs on RunPod GPU pods. Pulls `origin/main` at pod startup.\n- **`reigh-worker-orchestrator/`** \u2014 Python. Deployed on Railway from `megaplan/vibecomfy-sprint-07-orchestrator-pools-artifacts` branch.\n\nDatabase is Postgres on Supabase. Project ref `wczysqzxlwdndgxitrvc`. [REDACTED]:\n\n- `public.tasks` (rows have `route_key` column + `params` JSONB containing `route_contract` object)\n- `public.workers`\n- `public.route_backend_capabilities` (registry of what backends support which route_keys)\n- `public.route_backend_selectors`\n- RPCs: `claim_next_task_service_role`, `route_backend_claim_decision`, `count_queued_tasks_breakdown_service_role`, `derive_route_key` (does not yet exist; this sprint adds it)\n\nLive workers and orchestrator are running in production *right now*. [REDACTED]'s task chain (`0168dcc3-2a42-415d-84b5-a28cf6033850` + 6 children) is failed and poisoned with mismatched parent_route_key references \u2014 it stays Failed; don't try to revive it.\n\n## The three bugs that defined the diagnosis\n\n1. **Cross-repo deployment skew.** Orchestrator required `metadata.ready_for_tasks=true` for worker promotion. Worker-side publishing code sat in 169 unpushed commits on a feature branch. Production pods ran 8-day-old code, sat in `spawning` forever (heartbeat-guardian subprocess kept the DB row warm while the main process was dead), $0.69/hr \u00d7 18h = ~$12.75 of idle compute burned. The orchestrator's stuck-spawning detector existed but was gated on `queued_count > 0`, an empty queue silently disabled the lifecycle safety net.\n\n2. **Cross-layer contract gap.** The DB claim RPC requires every task to have non-null `route_key` and a `route_contract` object inside `params`. **Zero** of the resolvers in `supabase/functions/create-task/resolvers/` build a `route_contract` (`grep -rln route_contract` on the directory returns nothing relevant). Every production-side task ever created via the edge function has `route_key=NULL` and is categorically un-claimable. Live-test tasks work because they write to the DB through `reigh-worker/scripts/live_test/*` \u2014 a completely separate code path that does build the contract.\n\n3. **Cross-language drift.** `params.model_family` is overloaded with two incompatible enums: `{\"ltx\", \"wan\"}` (worker-internal classification used at `reigh-worker/source/task_handlers/tasks/task_registry.py:113, 300, 1157` for FPS / frame-step decisions) AND `{\"wan22_i2v\", \"wan22_vace\", \"ltx2\", \"ltx2_distilled\", \"qwen\", \"z_image\"}` (route-family for capability registry). The Python `_route_model_family` at `reigh-worker/source/task_handlers/tasks/template_routing.py:989` AND the TS `routeModelFamily` at `reigh-app/supabase/functions/_shared/selectedRoute.ts:445` both honor `params.model_family` as an explicit override before normalizing. The orchestrator handler at `reigh-worker/source/task_handlers/travel/orchestrator.py:436-437` writes the internal-namespace value into child task params, both ports faithfully respect it, route_keys come out as `model-wan` (not registered in capabilities), tasks are syntactically valid but semantically un-claimable.\n\nThe unifying meta-cause: the system has accumulated cross-boundary contracts (cross-repo, cross-layer, cross-language) faster than mechanisms to enforce equivalence across the boundary. Detection mechanisms exist (launcher preflight timeout, orchestrator's `Worker X: initializing (66087s)` log, DB RPCs' eligibility filters, worker's `validate_existing_child_route_contracts`) \u2014 all of them log negative verdicts; none of them escalate. Telemetry without teeth.\n\n## The chosen architectural direction (4 layers + cleanup)\n\nPick this over alternatives explicitly. Reasoning preserved so future readers know why.\n\n**Layer 0 \u2014 Cleanup (prerequisite).**\n\n**Layer 1 \u2014 DB rejects unclaimable inserts at write time.** A `BEFORE INSERT OR UPDATE` trigger on `tasks` calls `route_backend_claim_decision`; if the row is transitioning into status `Queued` and the RPC returns `eligible=false`, reject with a clear error. Exempt orchestrator task types (`task_type LIKE '%_orchestrator'`) from this \u2014 they are not claimed for inference and have no routing decision to make. \"Queued but categorically unclaimable\" becomes a database invariant violation rather than an operational state.\n\n**Layer 2 \u2014 Centralize route derivation in the DB.** Add two Postgres functions: `public.derive_route_key(task_type text, params jsonb) returns text` and `public.build_route_contract(task_type text, params jsonb) returns jsonb`. Port the logic from `_route_model_family`/`routeModelFamily` and friends. These become the **single source of truth** for route_key derivation. The TS edge function `supabase/functions/create-task/index.ts` calls them via `supabase.rpc(...)` between resolver and insert. The Python worker code that creates child tasks calls them via the same RPC path. Delete or shrink the local TS and Python implementations to thin RPC wrappers.\n\n**Layer 3 \u2014 Detectors that kill, not log.** Three specific conversions:\n\n- Worker launcher: the script that logs `\u274c Timed out waiting for worker preflight after 900s` must follow with `sys.exit(1)`. Find the script (it's in the worker repo or in the orchestrator's `worker_startup.template.sh`); turn the log into a gate.\n- `reigh-worker-orchestrator/gpu_orchestrator/worker_state.py:271-276` \u2014 `STARTUP_NEVER_READY` killer is gated on `queued_count > 0 AND not has_ever_claimed`. Drop the `queued_count` gate. An empty queue should NOT disable lifecycle safety.\n- `reigh-worker-orchestrator/gpu_orchestrator/control/phases/periodic.py:214` \u2014 the failsafe escape hatch `if is_active and not has_active_task and heartbeat_is_recent: continue`. Add an exception: don't short-circuit when `lifecycle == ACTIVE_INITIALIZING` past N seconds (e.g. 1800s). This is the actual line that let today's zombie live 18 hours.\n\n**Layer 4 \u2014 Sentinel cron.** One cron (Railway service or Supabase scheduled function) running every 60s. Queries `tasks` directly (not via the `count_queued_tasks_breakdown_service_role` RPC, which excludes orchestrator types and would be blind). Computes one state per tick: `OK | NO_WORK | UNCLAIMABLE_WORK | NO_READY_WORKERS | WORKERS_STUCK_INITIALIZING`. If `UNCLAIMABLE_WORK` OR `WORKERS_STUCK_INITIALIZING` persists for >5 minutes: page (Slack/Discord webhook is fine), AND tell the orchestrator to stop scaling for that pool until acknowledged. This is the catch-net for everything Layers 1-3 missed.\n\n## Cleanup required before Layers 1 and 2 land\n\nThese items would actively fight the new gates if not done first.\n\n**Data triage (before Layer 1's trigger goes live, otherwise the migration fails):**\n\n- Mark [REDACTED]'s parent `0168dcc3-2a42-415d-84b5-a28cf6033850` and her 6 children (`d2114387-b5dc-4521-b6a2-f2989379e891`, `79308f35-795c-4b1d-a8e5-7bdf8d82b64c`, `47dc41df-7656-4b88-a0e2-315e71939220`, `2b40a495-89cc-4bc4-a29e-515119bce2dd`, `a505b34e-3571-4abc-b29f-7ff773a8d0f2`, `cc83949d-8f24-407f-9b48-d7ca9962e410`) as terminally `Failed` with a clear reason; don't try to revive \u2014 they're poisoned with mismatched `parent_route_key` references.\n- The other ~40 stuck production tasks with `status='Queued' AND (selector_namespace IS NULL OR selector_namespace='production') AND route_key IS NULL` \u2014 either backfill them using the new `derive_route_key` DB function or terminally fail the ones older than a few hours.\n- `qwen_image_style` and `animate_character` route_keys return `eligible=false` from `route_backend_claim_decision` with reason `missing_capability` \u2014 their capability rows aren't in `route_backend_capabilities`. Add the missing capability rows in the same migration, OR fence those types out of the trigger pending registration. Don't ship the trigger with a known-broken task type.\n\n**Disambiguate `params.model_family` (prerequisite for Layer 2):**\n\nThe worker's internal code at `reigh-worker/source/task_handlers/tasks/task_registry.py:113, 117, 300-302, 1157-1163` uses `params.model_family \u2208 {ltx, wan}`. The route-namespace uses `{wan22_i2v, wan22_vace, ltx2, ltx2_distilled, qwen, z_image}`. Two enums sharing a key is the actual cause of bug #3. Rename the worker-internal field to `params.model_family_class` (or similar \u2014 pick one and apply). Leave `params.model_family` as either undefined or strictly route-namespace. Update all internal worker callsites. Then the DB function `derive_route_key` can read `model_name` cleanly without honoring a misleading override; or honor the override only if it's in the route-namespace enum.\n\n**Existing implementations to retire (Layer 2):**\n\nAfter the DB functions exist and the TS edge function calls them:\n\n- `reigh-app/supabase/functions/_shared/selectedRoute.ts` \u2014 `deriveRouteKey`, `routeModelFamily`, `routeGuidanceKey`, `routeContinuityCase`, `routeProfile`, etc. Delete OR shrink to a thin function that calls the RPC.\n- `reigh-app/supabase/functions/create-task/routeContract.ts` and `reigh-app/supabase/functions/_shared/routeContract.ts` \u2014 keep the validator surface but replace the contract-building logic with an RPC call.\n- `reigh-worker/source/task_handlers/tasks/template_routing.py` \u2014 keep contract-VALIDATION helpers; gut the derivation helpers. The worker no longer derives route_keys; it accepts them from the DB.\n- `reigh-worker/source/task_handlers/travel/orchestrator.py:436-437` \u2014 stop writing `params[\"model_family\"] = \"wan\"`. Either drop the write entirely OR write the route-namespace value derived from the DB function.\n\n**Concurrent-work hazard:**\n\nCurrent branches:\n- `reigh-app` is on `megaplan/vibecomfy-sprint-09-route-contract-shared` with uncommitted changes from an earlier subagent run in `routeContract.ts`/`routeContract.test.ts`/`resolvers/types.ts`, plus ~20 unrelated UI/feature edits that predate this session.\n- `reigh-worker` is at `c06fe1ab` on `origin/main` (was pushed in this session) on branch `megaplan/vibecomfy-sprint-09-control-rail-travel-matrix`.\n- `reigh-worker-orchestrator` is on `megaplan/vibecomfy-sprint-07-orchestrator-pools-artifacts` with 1 commit ahead/behind origin/main \u2014 Railway deploys from this branch.\n\n**Branch strategy** for this sprint:\n- Use a fresh branch off `origin/main` of each repo: `fix/contract-enforcement-layer1` etc.\n- Do NOT push to sprint-07 or sprint-09 branches.\n- The subagent's uncommitted WIP on `routeContract.ts` from the earlier session is partially aligned with Layer 2 but built local TS derivation rather than RPC calls. Discard those uncommitted edits OR stash them \u2014 they are superseded by Layer 2 which moves derivation to the DB.\n\n## Explicit non-goals (DO NOT do these in this sprint)\n\n- Do **not** build a `reigh-contracts` codegen package, JSON Schema source of truth, or cross-repo CI infrastructure. That is the right end state long-term but the wrong first move now. Reject any plan that proposes it.\n- Do **not** collapse the three repos into a monorepo. Same rationale.\n- Do **not** add PR checklists or release rituals. They get skipped under feature pressure.\n- Do **not** add new dashboards. The sentinel is the dashboard, and it pages.\n- Do **not** rewrite the orchestrator's lifecycle state machine. Just the three specific conversions in Layer 3.\n- Do **not** touch the live-test write path in `reigh-worker/scripts/live_test/*` beyond verifying its writes still satisfy the new DB trigger.\n- Do **not** revive [REDACTED]'s task chain. Mark it Failed and move on.\n- Do **not** ship Layer 1 (trigger) before Layer 0 (data triage) and Layer 2 (DB derive function) \u2014 order matters; the trigger calls the function, and existing rows must be valid against the function before the trigger lands.\n\n## Done criteria\n\nThe sprint is done when ALL of these are true:\n\n1. **New tasks can't be born unclaimable.** Inserting a `tasks` row with `status='Queued'` and no valid `route_key` / `route_contract` is rejected by the DB. Verified by attempting the insert via direct SQL and via the edge function \u2014 both fail with a clear error message naming the violated constraint.\n2. **Route derivation has one implementation.** A `derive_route_key(text, jsonb) returns text` function exists in Postgres. The TS edge function calls it via RPC. The Python worker either calls it via RPC (preferred) or has its derivation helpers reduced to thin wrappers. `grep -r 'routeModelFamily\\|_route_model_family' supabase/functions reigh-worker/source` should return zero meaningful matches outside of an RPC wrapper layer.\n3. **Today's specific bug #3 (`params.model_family` enum collision) is structurally impossible.** Either via the rename (worker-internal moved to `model_family_class`) or via the DB function ignoring out-of-enum overrides \u2014 but pick ONE and make it impossible for the bug to recur.\n4. **Pod launcher exits on preflight timeout.** Grep the launcher for the `Timed out waiting for worker preflight` string; the line directly after it is a process exit, not a continuation.\n5. **Orchestrator's `STARTUP_NEVER_READY` killer is queue-independent.** `worker_state.py:271-276` no longer gates on `queued_count > 0`.\n6. **Periodic failsafe escapes for `ACTIVE_INITIALIZING` past N seconds.** `periodic.py:214` has an exception clause; verify by reading the diff.\n7. **Sentinel cron is running.** A cron exists (Railway or Supabase scheduler), querying real DB state, emitting one of the five states. A test page fires when manually constructing a stuck state (e.g., insert a Queued task and confirm a `UNCLAIMABLE_WORK` page arrives within 5-6 min \u2014 then clean up).\n8. **A new freshly-created travel_orchestrator task runs end-to-end.** Through the edge function, claimed by a worker, fans out into segments, segments claimed and complete, parent marked Complete. Verifies the entire pipeline post-migration.\n9. **Three branches pushed to GitHub** (not merged to sprint branches), one per repo. Each PR description names the layer(s) it implements and references this brief.\n10. **No regression in live-test path.** Run one live-test pass after the changes ship; it succeeds.\n\n## Constraints, watchouts, environment\n\n- **DB connection**: use the Supabase project at `https://[REDACTED]`. Service role key lives in `~/.hermes/.env` as `SUPABASE_SERVICE_ROLE_KEY` (also in each repo's `.env`).\n- **Python environment**: every Python invocation in the orchestrator scripts requires `PYENV_VERSION=3.11.11` because the shebang's pyenv version (3.8.10) isn't installed. The wrapper has been updated to auto-set this; verify it works.\n- **Migrations**: deploy via `npx supabase db push --linked` (never `db reset --linked`). Edge functions via `npx supabase functions deploy <name> --project-ref wczysqzxlwdndgxitrvc`. Both repos already have these commands working.\n- **Railway**: orchestrator auto-deploys on push to its tracked branch. Don't push the new branch to that tracked branch \u2014 open it as a separate branch and the user merges manually.\n- **RunPod pods**: workers pull `origin/main` of `reigh-worker` at pod spawn. After Layer 2 ships, the new pods will pull the updated code; existing pods need to be terminated and let the orchestrator respawn (or done manually via `./debug kill <worker_id>` from the workspace root).\n- **Existing tooling worth using**: `./debug worker <id>` (new in this session, prints lifecycle, code-drift, why-not-killed), `./debug deployment-drift` (compares local vs origin vs Railway vs live pod SHAs). Both are uncommitted in `reigh-worker-orchestrator/scripts/`. Commit them as part of this sprint.\n\n## Out-of-scope follow-ups (file as separate sprints, do not pursue in this run)\n\n- The `reigh-contracts` codegen package and cross-repo CI (Codex's \"do it right\" architecture).\n- Worker SHA pinning at pod spawn (replace `git reset --hard origin/main` with checkout of an explicit SHA recorded by orchestrator on spawn).\n- Collapsing the three repos.\n- Cleaning up the 36 stale \"ignored worker(s) for production capacity control\" rows.\n- Repairing the broken `./debug pipeline` command.\n- The dual-implementation problem more broadly \u2014 Layer 2 only collapses route derivation; heartbeat formats, task params validators, edge function input shapes are all still independent.\n\n## Style notes for the plan and execution\n\n- File paths in any code change should be absolute (e.g. `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-worker/source/...`) or repo-relative with the repo name as the first segment.\n- Migrations should be additive and reversible where possible.\n- The DB trigger should have a clear `RAISE EXCEPTION` message naming the violated invariant (e.g. `'route_contract validation failed: %s', decision_reason`).\n- Don't add dependencies. Use what's already in each repo.\n- The sentinel can be a tiny Python script wrapped in a Railway cron job, or a Supabase Edge Function on a schedule \u2014 either is fine; pick the one with the smallest new infrastructure.\n\nThe brief is the dominant variable. If something here is ambiguous, prefer the most conservative interpretation that still moves the bug class structurally. If something is contradictory, flag it and ask before proceeding.",

        - Update the plan to address the significant issues.
        - Keep the plan readable and executable.
        - Return flags_addressed with the exact flag IDs you addressed.
        - Include `changes_summary` as a short plain-English summary of what changed in the revision. If there were no concrete flags, say that explicitly (for example: `No critique flags were raised; refined wording and kept the plan aligned for execution.`).
        - Preserve or improve success criteria quality. Each criterion must have a `priority` of `must`, `should`, or `info`. Promote or demote priorities if critique feedback reveals a criterion was over- or under-weighted.
        - Each success criterion should include a `requires` field listing the capabilities needed for verification. Valid capability strings: `run_shell`, `read_files`, `run_tests`, `parse_diff`, `read_build_output`, `run_linter` (container), `drive_browser`, `inspect_runtime_ui`, `observe_runtime_logs`, `subjective_judgment`, `verify_physical_device` (human). `must` criteria MUST have non-empty `requires`. Example: `{"criterion": "All tests pass", "priority": "must", "requires": ["run_tests"]}`.
        - Verify that the plan remains aligned with the user's original intent, not just internal plan quality.
        - Remove unjustified scope growth. If critique raised scope creep, narrow the plan back to the original idea unless the broader work is strictly required.
        - Maintain the structural template: H1 title, ## Overview, phase sections with numbered step sections, ## Execution Order or ## Validation Order.
        - CRITICAL: Your entire revised plan markdown (all sections) must be output as the `plan` field in the structured output. The prose response must not contain the plan text.
        - CRITICAL: Return only the structured JSON object for the schema fields `plan`, `changes_summary`, `flags_addressed`, `assumptions`, `success_criteria`, and `questions`. Do not add commentary before or after the JSON object.

        Plan template — simple format (adapt to the actual repo and scope):
````md
# Implementation Plan: [Title]

## Overview
Summarize the goal, current repository shape, and the constraints that matter.

## Main Phase

### Step 1: Audit the current behavior (`megaplan/prompts.py`)
**Scope:** Small
1. **Inspect** the current implementation and call out the exact insertion points (`megaplan/prompts.py:29`).

### Step 2: Add the first change (`megaplan/evaluation.py`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. **Implement** the smallest viable change with exact file references (`megaplan/evaluation.py:1`).
2. **Capture** any tricky behavior with a short example.
   ```python
   issues = validate_plan_structure(plan_text)
   ```

### Step 3: Wire downstream behavior (`megaplan/handlers.py`, `megaplan/workers.py`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. **Update** the runtime flow in the touched files (`megaplan/handlers.py:400`, `megaplan/workers.py:199`).

### Step 4: Prove the change (`tests/test_evaluation.py`, `tests/test_megaplan.py`)
**Scope:** Small
1. **Run** the cheapest targeted checks first (`tests/test_evaluation.py:1`).
2. **Finish** with broader verification once the wiring is in place (`tests/test_megaplan.py:1`).

## Execution Order
1. Update prompts and mocks before enforcing stricter validation.
2. Land higher-risk wiring after the validator and tests are ready.

## Validation Order
1. Start with focused unit tests.
2. Run the broader suite after the flow changes are in place.
````

For complex plans, use multiple phases:
````md
## Phase 1: Foundation — Dependencies, DB, Types

### Step 1: Install dependencies (`package.json`)
...

### Step 2: Create database migration (`supabase/migrations/`)
...

## Phase 2: Core Integration

### Step 3: Port the main component (`src/components/`)
...
````

Template guidance:
- Simple plans: use `## Main Phase` with `### Step N:` sections underneath.
- Complex plans: use multiple `## Phase N:` sections, each containing `### Step N:` steps. Step numbers are global (not per-phase).
- The flat `## Step N:` format (without phases) also works for backwards compatibility.
- Key invariants: one H1 title, one `## Overview`, numbered step sections (`### Step N:` or `## Step N:`), and at least one ordering section.

(source: reigh-app/supabase/functions/create-task/resolvers/ and routeContract.test.ts; relevance: medium)
- Branch posture per the brief: each repo currently has unrelated WIP on its sprint branch. The plan must explicitly branch fresh from origin/main of each repo (fix/contract-enforcement-*) and not push to sprint-07/sprint-09 branches because Railway auto-deploys from sprint-07 in the orchestrator and there is concurrent uncommitted work in reigh-app on sprint-09 (routeContract.ts WIP plus ~20 unrelated UI edits). (source: Task brief 'Concurrent-work hazard' + gitStatus showing docs/vibecomfy-post-chain-qwen-proof branch; relevance: high)
- Live-test path at reigh-worker/scripts/live_test/matrix.py builds route_contract via `_route_contract()` (line 627) writing the same `{route_selection_snapshot: ...}` shape. After Layer 1 ships, this insert path must continue to produce rows that pass the trigger — explicitly listed in the Done Criteria as 'no regression in live-test path'. (source: reigh-worker/scripts/live_test/matrix.py:627; relevance: high)
- Worker preflight script search produced `reigh-worker/scripts/live_test/preflight.py` (a safety gate for live-test, not pod launcher). The pod launcher logic likely lives in `reigh-worker-orchestrator` worker_startup.template.sh or in a script invoked by it. Need to locate it precisely before claiming the launcher-exit conversion is done. (source: Explore subagent search result; relevance: medium)
- Orchestrator detector at worker_state.py:271-276 currently reads: `if queued_count > 0 and not has_ever_claimed and effective_age_sec > config.startup_grace_period_sec: terminate('STARTUP_NEVER_READY')`. Dropping queued_count gate is a one-line change but config.startup_grace_period_sec must be a sensible value for the empty-queue case (e.g., not so short it flaps when queue genuinely empty during normal pod warmup). Adding the ACTIVE_INITIALIZING-past-N-seconds exception requires reading `ws.lifecycle` and the age — confirm WorkerState exposes lifecycle + initialized_at/spawned_at on `ws` before assuming the diff is one-line. The brief also flags ~40 other Queued+route_key=NULL rows for backfill-or-fail. The trigger uses BEFORE INSERT OR UPDATE so existing rows are safe UNTIL someone updates them, but a Layer-0 sweep is still required for the 'new freshly-created travel_orchestrator end-to-end' done-criterion to pass cleanly. - The 20260513 migration uses `NOT VALID` to skip retroactive validation of ~45k legacy Complete/Failed rows. Layer 1's new trigger should follow the same pattern (BEFORE INSERT OR UPDATE WHEN NEW.status = 'Queued') to avoid scanning legacy rows, and must exempt `task_type LIKE '%_orchestrator'` as the brief specifies. (source: reigh-app/supabase/migrations/20260513111812_require_route_key.sql lines 10-13; relevance: high)

        - [pass_to_pass] reigh-app/supabase/functions/create-task/routeContract.test.ts (existing): stampTaskRouteContract output shape, ROUTE_CONTRACT_PARAM_KEY normalization, route_key + selector mirroring onto top-level columns.

- [fail_to_pass] NEW migration test: insert Queued task with route_key set but no capability row should ERROR (or be exempted if task_type LIKE '%_orchestrator'): Layer 1 trigger calls route_backend_claim_decision and rejects when eligible=false; orchestrator types exempt.
- [fail_to_pass] NEW DB function test: derive_route_key(task_type, params) returns same value as existing TS deriveRouteKey for a fixture set: Layer 2 derive_route_key matches current TS+Python derivation across enumerated cases (wan22_i2v, wan22_vace, ltx2, ltx2_distilled, qwen, z_image, plus the orchestrator parent case).

        ### Constraints
        - Do NOT build a reigh-contracts codegen package, JSON Schema source of truth, or cross-repo CI. Explicit non-goal.
- Do NOT collapse repos into a monorepo. Explicit non-goal.
- Do NOT push to existing sprint branches (megaplan/vibecomfy-sprint-07-* or megaplan/vibecomfy-sprint-09-*). Use fresh fix/contract-enforcement-* branches off origin/main of each repo. Railway auto-deploys from sprint-07 of the orchestrator — pushing there is destructive.
- Do NOT revive [REDACTED]'s parent task chain (0168dcc3-2a42-415d-84b5-a28cf6033850 + 6 named children). Mark Failed.
- Do NOT touch live-test write path (reigh-worker/scripts/live_test/*) beyond ensuring it still satisfies the new trigger and the model_family rename.
- Do NOT rewrite orchestrator lifecycle state machine. Only the three specific Layer 3 conversions.
- Migrations: deploy via `npx supabase db push --linked`. NEVER `db reset --linked`.
- Edge functions: deploy individually via `npx supabase functions deploy <name> --project-ref wczysqzxlwdndgxitrvc`.
- Layer ordering is hard: Layer 0 (cleanup) → Layer 2 (derive_route_key function) → Layer 1 (trigger that calls it) → Layer 3 → Layer 4. Trigger calls function; existing rows must validate before trigger lands.
- Layer 1 trigger must exempt `task_type LIKE '%_orchestrator'` (they have no routing decision).
- Layer 1 trigger must use BEFORE INSERT OR UPDATE WHEN NEW.status = 'Queued' (do not scan legacy ~45k rows).
- qwen_image_style and animate_character route_keys currently return eligible=false (missing_capability). Either add capability rows in the same migration OR fence those types out of the trigger — must not ship trigger with known-broken types.
- Python invocations in orchestrator scripts require PYENV_VERSION=3.11.11 (pyenv 3.8.10 isn't installed). Wrapper auto-sets it — verify before running anything.
- Pick exactly ONE resolution for the model_family enum collision: rename worker-internal to model_family_class, OR have derive_route_key ignore out-of-route-namespace overrides. Don't ship both — clarity matters more than defense-in-depth here.
- Add no new dependencies. Use what's already in each repo.
- All file paths in plan/execution should be absolute or repo-name-prefixed.
- Migrations should be additive and reversible where possible; DB trigger error must use RAISE EXCEPTION naming the violated invariant.
- Three PRs (one per repo), pushed but not merged; description names the layer(s) implemented and references this brief.
- Layer 4 sentinel queries tasks DIRECTLY (not via count_queued_tasks_breakdown_service_role RPC, which excludes orchestrator types).

        ### Suggested Approach
        Sequence by dependency, not by layer number. Three repos, three PRs, one merged-by-user.

**Phase A — reigh-app (DB + edge function, single PR fix/contract-enforcement-db):**
1. Branch fresh from origin/main of reigh-app. Stash or discard sprint-09 WIP on routeContract.ts/routeContract.test.ts/resolvers/types.ts before branching — those edits build local TS derivation, which Layer 2 supersedes.
2. Migration N+1: add `public.derive_route_key(task_type text, params jsonb) returns text` and `public.build_route_contract(task_type text, params jsonb) returns jsonb`. Port logic from `_shared/selectedRoute.ts` (routeModelFamily 445, routeGuidanceKey 490, routeContinuityCase 499, routeProfile 506, deriveRouteKey 238). Either reject out-of-route-namespace `model_family` overrides OR read from a new `model_family_class` key — pick ignore-overrides for the DB side (less coupling to worker rename timing).
3. Migration N+2: data triage. Mark [REDACTED]'s 7-task chain Failed with reason. Run `derive_route_key` over the ~40 stuck route_key=NULL Queued rows; backfill those that resolve cleanly, mark the rest Failed (UPDATE doesn't trip the not-yet-deployed trigger). Insert missing `route_backend_capabilities` rows for `qwen_image_style` and `animate_character` (or document the fence-out decision).
4. Migration N+3: BEFORE INSERT OR UPDATE trigger on `tasks` WHEN `NEW.status = 'Queued' AND NEW.task_type NOT LIKE '%_orchestrator'` that calls `route_backend_claim_decision`; RAISE EXCEPTION with the decision's reason if `eligible=false`. Companion: RAISE EXCEPTION if `params->'route_contract'` is missing/empty.
5. Rewrite `stampTaskRouteContract` in `routeContract.ts:150` to call `supabase.rpc('build_route_contract', {...})` instead of the local routeSnapshotFields. Reduce `_shared/selectedRoute.ts` derivation helpers to thin RPC wrappers (or delete the now-unused private ones). Keep `validateCandidate` and `readRouteContractFromParams` — validation stays at the language boundary.
6. Update `routeContract.test.ts` to mock the RPC call. Verify `claim-next-task/index.test.ts` still passes.
7. Deploy: `npx supabase db push --linked` then `npx supabase functions deploy create-task --project-ref wczysqzxlwdndgxitrvc`.

**Phase B — reigh-worker (Layer 0 rename + retire derivation, branch fix/contract-enforcement-worker):**
1. Decision required before coding: rename worker-internal field. Confirm choice (`model_family_class`?) by asking user — affects ~8 callsites and fixture files. Default to `model_family_class`.
2. Replace `_route_model_family` (template_routing.py:989) and friends with thin wrappers that read the route-contract object the DB already stamped onto each task (the contract arrives as part of the task row; worker doesn't need to re-derive). Keep `validate_existing_child_route_contracts` and other contract-VALIDATION helpers.
3. orchestrator.py:436-437 — stop writing `model_family` under the route-namespace overload. Either drop the write or write under `model_family_class`. (If keeping for back-compat: pin it to the worker-internal namespace name explicitly.)
4. Update `task_registry.py:113-117, 300-302, 1157-1163` and `matrix.py:197, 205, 436, 463` and `test_primitives.py` to read `model_family_class`.

**Phase C — reigh-worker-orchestrator (Layer 3 conversions + Layer 4 sentinel, branch fix/contract-enforcement-orchestrator):**
1. worker_state.py:271-276 — drop `queued_count > 0` gate.
2. periodic.py:214 — add exception: when `ws.lifecycle == ACTIVE_INITIALIZING` and `age > 1800s`, don't short-circuit. Verify WorkerState exposes `lifecycle` + age field; if not, add accessor.
3. Pod-launcher preflight-timeout exit: find the actual call site (likely in `worker_startup.template.sh` or a Python script it invokes — grep `preflight` not the missing 900s string). Confirm site with user before patching; convert log to `sys.exit(1)`.
4. Commit the uncommitted debug-CLI scripts per brief (verify they actually are uncommitted — Explore reports tree clean, so this may already be done; if so, skip).
5. Layer 4 sentinel: implement as Supabase scheduled edge function (smaller footprint than new Railway service). New function `supabase/functions/route-contract-sentinel/index.ts` invoked by `pg_cron` every 60s. Queries `tasks` directly; classifies state; on persistent UNCLAIMABLE_WORK / WORKERS_STUCK_INITIALIZING (>5min), POSTs to Slack/Discord webhook (URL via env) and inserts a 'pause-scaling' row that the orchestrator reads each tick. Add a small read in orchestrator periodic phase that respects the pause row.

**Phase D — verification:**
1. Run a fresh travel_orchestrator end-to-end through the production edge function. Must claim, fanout, complete.
2. Run reigh-worker/scripts/live_test once. Must succeed.
3. Construct a stuck-Queued fixture; confirm sentinel pages within 5-6 min; clean up.
4. Verify trigger rejects bad inserts via direct SQL.
5. Open three PRs (not merged); each description names layers implemented + links this brief.

**Sequencing-sensitive callouts:**
- Phase A.3 (data triage) MUST land before Phase A.4 (trigger) or the trigger's first conflict will be with poisoned rows under UPDATE.
- Phase A.2 (derive_route_key function) MUST land before Phase A.4 (trigger that calls it via route_backend_claim_decision).
- Phase B (worker rename) and Phase C (orchestrator) are independent and can run in parallel after Phase A is deployed.
- Pods need to be cycled after Phase B's reigh-worker push since they pull origin/main at spawn (`./debug kill <id>` or terminate via orchestrator).

**Open questions to flag before starting:**
1. Pod-launcher timeout string — confirm where it lives (different wording? deleted? different repo?). Default to grepping 'preflight' broadly.
2. model_family resolution — confirm `model_family_class` rename vs DB-side ignore. Brief says 'pick one'; prefer to pick before planning Phase B.
3. `route_backend_capabilities` table schema — query live DB before writing the trigger (need column names for the call to route_backend_claim_decision). Missing capability rows for qwen_image_style / animate_character — confirm columns before writing the INSERT.
4. Sentinel target: Supabase scheduled function vs Railway cron — recommend Supabase (smaller footprint, one repo); brief permits either.
        The engineering brief above was produced by analyzing the codebase. Use it as a strong starting point, but the suggested approach is a hypothesis — verify it's the best fix, not just a valid one.

        Idea:
# Brief: stop the cross-boundary contract bleeding

[all-codex/standard +prep]

## Background and goal

Reigh is a production AI/ML task system split across three independently deployed surfaces speaking an implicit wire-level protocol with no contract definition, no codegen, no version negotiation. In one debugging session today the team hit three different flavors of the same class of bug — each one an *asymmetric contract evolution* that produced silent runtime failures. This is the fourth documented recurrence in five months; the same engineer has now introduced and silently broken the same `ready_for_tasks` contract twice, four months apart.

Goal of this sprint: ship a coherent set of guardrails that make this class of bug **structurally loud** (and in two specific places, structurally impossible). Not perfect prevention. Bleeding stopped, recurrence cost capped, future change shapes safer.

The fix is being shipped at runtime via the existing Postgres surface plus a small operational sentinel — explicitly NOT via a build-time codegen contracts package. That package may be the right end state in 6+ months; it is the wrong first move for one solo engineer in feature-shipping mode. Stop the bleeding first; centralize at the layer that already has a chokepoint; defer the perfect-protocol design.

## System under change

Three repos in `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh-workspace/`:

- **`reigh-app/`** — TypeScript. Supabase edge functions, DB migrations, frontend.
- **`reigh-worker/`** — Python. Runs on RunPod GPU pods. Pulls `origin/main` at pod startup.
- **`reigh-worker-orchestrator/`** — Python. Deployed on Railway from `megaplan/vibecomfy-sprint-07-orchestrator-pools-artifacts` branch.

Database is Postgres on Supabase. Project ref `wczysqzxlwdndgxitrvc`. [REDACTED]:

- `public.tasks` (rows have `route_key` column + `params` JSONB containing `route_contract` object)
- `public.workers`
- `public.route_backend_capabilities` (registry of what backends support which route_keys)
- `public.route_backend_selectors`
- RPCs: `claim_next_task_service_role`, `route_backend_claim_decision`, `count_queued_tasks_breakdown_service_role`, `derive_route_key` (does not yet exist; this sprint adds it)

Live workers and orchestrator are running in production *right now*. [REDACTED]'s task chain (`0168dcc3-2a42-415d-84b5-a28cf6033850` + 6 children) is failed and poisoned with mismatched parent_route_key references — it stays Failed; don't try to revive it.

## The three bugs that defined the diagnosis

1. **Cross-repo deployment skew.** Orchestrator required `metadata.ready_for_tasks=true` for worker promotion. Worker-side publishing code sat in 169 unpushed commits on a feature branch. Production pods ran 8-day-old code, sat in `spawning` forever (heartbeat-guardian subprocess kept the DB row warm while the main process was dead), $0.69/hr × 18h = ~$12.75 of idle compute burned. The orchestrator's stuck-spawning detector existed but was gated on `queued_count > 0`, an empty queue silently disabled the lifecycle safety net.

2. **Cross-layer contract gap.** The DB claim RPC requires every task to have non-null `route_key` and a `route_contract` object inside `params`. **Zero** of the resolvers in `supabase/functions/create-task/resolvers/` build a `route_contract` (`grep -rln route_contract` on the directory returns nothing relevant). Every production-side task ever created via the edge function has `route_key=NULL` and is categorically un-claimable. Live-test tasks work because they write to the DB through `reigh-worker/scripts/live_test/*` — a completely separate code path that does build the contract.

3. **Cross-language drift.** `params.model_family` is overloaded with two incompatible enums: `{"ltx", "wan"}` (worker-internal classification used at `reigh-worker/source/task_handlers/tasks/task_registry.py:113, 300, 1157` for FPS / frame-step decisions) AND `{"wan22_i2v", "wan22_vace", "ltx2", "ltx2_distilled", "qwen", "z_image"}` (route-family for capability registry). The Python `_route_model_family` at `reigh-worker/source/task_handlers/tasks/template_routing.py:989` AND the TS `routeModelFamily` at `reigh-app/supabase/functions/_shared/selectedRoute.ts:445` both honor `params.model_family` as an explicit override before normalizing. The orchestrator handler at `reigh-worker/source/task_handlers/travel/orchestrator.py:436-437` writes the internal-namespace value into child task params, both ports faithfully respect it, route_keys come out as `model-wan` (not registered in capabilities), tasks are syntactically valid but semantically un-claimable.

The unifying meta-cause: the system has accumulated cross-boundary contracts (cross-repo, cross-layer, cross-language) faster than mechanisms to enforce equivalence across the boundary. Detection mechanisms exist (launcher preflight timeout, orchestrator's `Worker X: initializing (66087s)` log, DB RPCs' eligibility filters, worker's `validate_existing_child_route_contracts`) — all of them log negative verdicts; none of them escalate. Telemetry without teeth.

## The chosen architectural direction (4 layers + cleanup)

Pick this over alternatives explicitly. Reasoning preserved so future readers know why.

**Layer 0 — Cleanup (prerequisite).**

**Layer 1 — DB rejects unclaimable inserts at write time.** A `BEFORE INSERT OR UPDATE` trigger on `tasks` calls `route_backend_claim_decision`; if the row is transitioning into status `Queued` and the RPC returns `eligible=false`, reject with a clear error. Exempt orchestrator task types (`task_type LIKE '%_orchestrator'`) from this — they are not claimed for inference and have no routing decision to make. "Queued but categorically unclaimable" becomes a database invariant violation rather than an operational state.

**Layer 2 — Centralize route derivation in the DB.** Add two Postgres functions: `public.derive_route_key(task_type text, params jsonb) returns text` and `public.build_route_contract(task_type text, params jsonb) returns jsonb`. Port the logic from `_route_model_family`/`routeModelFamily` and friends. These become the **single source of truth** for route_key derivation. The TS edge function `supabase/functions/create-task/index.ts` calls them via `supabase.rpc(...)` between resolver and insert. The Python worker code that creates child tasks calls them via the same RPC path. Delete or shrink the local TS and Python implementations to thin RPC wrappers.

**Layer 3 — Detectors that kill, not log.** Three specific conversions:

- Worker launcher: the script that logs `❌ Timed out waiting for worker preflight after 900s` must follow with `sys.exit(1)`. Find the script (it's in the worker repo or in the orchestrator's `worker_startup.template.sh`); turn the log into a gate.
- `reigh-worker-orchestrator/gpu_orchestrator/worker_state.py:271-276` — `STARTUP_NEVER_READY` killer is gated on `queued_count > 0 AND not has_ever_claimed`. Drop the `queued_count` gate. An empty queue should NOT disable lifecycle safety.
- `reigh-worker-orchestrator/gpu_orchestrator/control/phases/periodic.py:214` — the failsafe escape hatch `if is_active and not has_active_task and heartbeat_is_recent: continue`. Add an exception: don't short-circuit when `lifecycle == ACTIVE_INITIALIZING` past N seconds (e.g. 1800s). This is the actual line that let today's zombie live 18 hours.

**Layer 4 — Sentinel cron.** One cron (Railway service or Supabase scheduled function) running every 60s. Queries `tasks` directly (not via the `count_queued_tasks_breakdown_service_role` RPC, which excludes orchestrator types and would be blind). Computes one state per tick: `OK | NO_WORK | UNCLAIMABLE_WORK | NO_READY_WORKERS | WORKERS_STUCK_INITIALIZING`. If `UNCLAIMABLE_WORK` OR `WORKERS_STUCK_INITIALIZING` persists for >5 minutes: page (Slack/Discord webhook is fine), AND tell the orchestrator to stop scaling for that pool until acknowledged. This is the catch-net for everything Layers 1-3 missed.

## Cleanup required before Layers 1 and 2 land

These items would actively fight the new gates if not done first.

**Data triage (before Layer 1's trigger goes live, otherwise the migration fails):**

- Mark [REDACTED]'s parent `0168dcc3-2a42-415d-84b5-a28cf6033850` and her 6 children (`d2114387-b5dc-4521-b6a2-f2989379e891`, `79308f35-795c-4b1d-a8e5-7bdf8d82b64c`, `47dc41df-7656-4b88-a0e2-315e71939220`, `2b40a495-89cc-4bc4-a29e-515119bce2dd`, `a505b34e-3571-4abc-b29f-7ff773a8d0f2`, `cc83949d-8f24-407f-9b48-d7ca9962e410`) as terminally `Failed` with a clear reason; don't try to revive — they're poisoned with mismatched `parent_route_key` references.
- The other ~40 stuck production tasks with `status='Queued' AND (selector_namespace IS NULL OR selector_namespace='production') AND route_key IS NULL` — either backfill them using the new `derive_route_key` DB function or terminally fail the ones older than a few hours.
- `qwen_image_style` and `animate_character` route_keys return `eligible=false` from `route_backend_claim_decision` with reason `missing_capability` — their capability rows aren't in `route_backend_capabilities`. Add the missing capability rows in the same migration, OR fence those types out of the trigger pending registration. Don't ship the trigger with a known-broken task type.

**Disambiguate `params.model_family` (prerequisite for Layer 2):**

The worker's internal code at `reigh-worker/source/task_handlers/tasks/task_registry.py:113, 117, 300-302, 1157-1163` uses `params.model_family ∈ {ltx, wan}`. The route-namespace uses `{wan22_i2v, wan22_vace, ltx2, ltx2_distilled, qwen, z_image}`. Two enums sharing a key is the actual cause of bug #3. Rename the worker-internal field to `params.model_family_class` (or similar — pick one and apply). Leave `params.model_family` as either undefined or strictly route-namespace. Update all internal worker callsites. Then the DB function `derive_route_key` can read `model_name` cleanly without honoring a misleading override; or honor the override only if it's in the route-namespace enum.

**Existing implementations to retire (Layer 2):**

After the DB functions exist and the TS edge function calls them:

- `reigh-app/supabase/functions/_shared/selectedRoute.ts` — `deriveRouteKey`, `routeModelFamily`, `routeGuidanceKey`, `routeContinuityCase`, `routeProfile`, etc. Delete OR shrink to a thin function that calls the RPC.
- `reigh-app/supabase/functions/create-task/routeContract.ts` and `reigh-app/supabase/functions/_shared/routeContract.ts` — keep the validator surface but replace the contract-building logic with an RPC call.
- `reigh-worker/source/task_handlers/tasks/template_routing.py` — keep contract-VALIDATION helpers; gut the derivation helpers. The worker no longer derives route_keys; it accepts them from the DB.
- `reigh-worker/source/task_handlers/travel/orchestrator.py:436-437` — stop writing `params["model_family"] = "wan"`. Either drop the write entirely OR write the route-namespace value derived from the DB function.

**Concurrent-work hazard:**

Current branches:
- `reigh-app` is on `megaplan/vibecomfy-sprint-09-route-contract-shared` with uncommitted changes from an earlier subagent run in `routeContract.ts`/`routeContract.test.ts`/`resolvers/types.ts`, plus ~20 unrelated UI/feature edits that predate this session.
- `reigh-worker` is at `c06fe1ab` on `origin/main` (was pushed in this session) on branch `megaplan/vibecomfy-sprint-09-control-rail-travel-matrix`.
- `reigh-worker-orchestrator` is on `megaplan/vibecomfy-sprint-07-orchestrator-pools-artifacts` with 1 commit ahead/behind origin/main — Railway deploys from this branch.

**Branch strategy** for this sprint:
- Use a fresh branch off `origin/main` of each repo: `fix/contract-enforcement-layer1` etc.
- Do NOT push to sprint-07 or sprint-09 branches.
- The subagent's uncommitted WIP on `routeContract.ts` from the earlier session is partially aligned with Layer 2 but built local TS derivation rather than RPC calls. Discard those uncommitted edits OR stash them — they are superseded by Layer 2 which moves derivation to the DB.

## Explicit non-goals (DO NOT do these in this sprint)

- Do **not** build a `reigh-contracts` codegen package, JSON Schema source of truth, or cross-repo CI infrastructure. That is the right end state long-term but the wrong first move now. Reject any plan that proposes it.
- Do **not** collapse the three repos into a monorepo. Same rationale.
- Do **not** add PR checklists or release rituals. They get skipped under feature pressure.
- Do **not** add new dashboards. The sentinel is the dashboard, and it pages.
- Do **not** rewrite the orchestrator's lifecycle state machine. Just the three specific conversions in Layer 3.
- Do **not** touch the live-test write path in `reigh-worker/scripts/live_test/*` beyond verifying its writes still satisfy the new DB trigger.
- Do **not** revive [REDACTED]'s task chain. Mark it Failed and move on.
- Do **not** ship Layer 1 (trigger) before Layer 0 (data triage) and Layer 2 (DB derive function) — order matters; the trigger calls the function, and existing rows must be valid against the function before the trigger lands.

## Done criteria

The sprint is done when ALL of these are true:

1. **New tasks can't be born unclaimable.** Inserting a `tasks` row with `status='Queued'` and no valid `route_key` / `route_contract` is rejected by the DB. Verified by attempting the insert via direct SQL and via the edge function — both fail with a clear error message naming the violated constraint.
2. **Route derivation has one implementation.** A `derive_route_key(text, jsonb) returns text` function exists in Postgres. The TS edge function calls it via RPC. The Python worker either calls it via RPC (preferred) or has its derivation helpers reduced to thin wrappers. `grep -r 'routeModelFamily\|_route_model_family' supabase/functions reigh-worker/source` should return zero meaningful matches outside of an RPC wrapper layer.
3. **Today's specific bug #3 (`params.model_family` enum collision) is structurally impossible.** Either via the rename (worker-internal moved to `model_family_class`) or via the DB function ignoring out-of-enum overrides — but pick ONE and make it impossible for the bug to recur.
4. **Pod launcher exits on preflight timeout.** Grep the launcher for the `Timed out waiting for worker preflight` string; the line directly after it is a process exit, not a continuation.
5. **Orchestrator's `STARTUP_NEVER_READY` killer is queue-independent.** `worker_state.py:271-276` no longer gates on `queued_count > 0`.
6. **Periodic failsafe escapes for `ACTIVE_INITIALIZING` past N seconds.** `periodic.py:214` has an exception clause; verify by reading the diff.
7. **Sentinel cron is running.** A cron exists (Railway or Supabase scheduler), querying real DB state, emitting one of the five states. A test page fires when manually constructing a stuck state (e.g., insert a Queued task and confirm a `UNCLAIMABLE_WORK` page arrives within 5-6 min — then clean up).
8. **A new freshly-created travel_orchestrator task runs end-to-end.** Through the edge function, claimed by a worker, fans out into segments, segments claimed and complete, parent marked Complete. Verifies the entire pipeline post-migration.
9. **Three branches pushed to GitHub** (not merged to sprint branches), one per repo. Each PR description names the layer(s) it implements and references this brief.
10. **No regression in live-test path.** Run one live-test pass after the changes ship; it succeeds.

## Constraints, watchouts, environment

- **DB connection**: use the Supabase project at `https://[REDACTED]`. Service role key lives in `~/.hermes/.env` as `SUPABASE_SERVICE_ROLE_KEY` (also in each repo's `.env`).
- **Python environment**: every Python invocation in the orchestrator scripts requires `PYENV_VERSION=3.11.11` because the shebang's pyenv version (3.8.10) isn't installed. The wrapper has been updated to auto-set this; verify it works.
- **Migrations**: deploy via `npx supabase db push --linked` (never `db reset --linked`). Edge functions via `npx supabase functions deploy <name> --project-ref wczysqzxlwdndgxitrvc`. Both repos already have these commands working.
- **Railway**: orchestrator auto-deploys on push to its tracked branch. Don't push the new branch to that tracked branch — open it as a separate branch and the user merges manually.
- **RunPod pods**: workers pull `origin/main` of `reigh-worker` at pod spawn. After Layer 2 ships, the new pods will pull the updated code; existing pods need to be terminated and let the orchestrator respawn (or done manually via `./debug kill <worker_id>` from the workspace root).
- **Existing tooling worth using**: `./debug worker <id>` (new in this session, prints lifecycle, code-drift, why-not-killed), `./debug deployment-drift` (compares local vs origin vs Railway vs live pod SHAs). Both are uncommitted in `reigh-worker-orchestrator/scripts/`. Commit them as part of this sprint.

## Out-of-scope follow-ups (file as separate sprints, do not pursue in this run)

- The `reigh-contracts` codegen package and cross-repo CI (Codex's "do it right" architecture).
- Worker SHA pinning at pod spawn (replace `git reset --hard origin/main` with checkout of an explicit SHA recorded by orchestrator on spawn).
- Collapsing the three repos.
- Cleaning up the 36 stale "ignored worker(s) for production capacity control" rows.
- Repairing the broken `./debug pipeline` command.
- The dual-implementation problem more broadly — Layer 2 only collapses route derivation; heartbeat formats, task params validators, edge function input shapes are all still independent.

## Style notes for the plan and execution

- File paths in any code change should be absolute (e.g. `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-worker/source/...`) or repo-relative with the repo name as the first segment.
- Migrations should be additive and reversible where possible.
- The DB trigger should have a clear `RAISE EXCEPTION` message naming the violated invariant (e.g. `'route_contract validation failed: %s', decision_reason`).
- Don't add dependencies. Use what's already in each repo.
- The sentinel can be a tiny Python script wrapped in a Railway cron job, or a Supabase Edge Function on a schedule — either is fine; pick the one with the smallest new infrastructure.

The brief is the dominant variable. If something here is ambiguous, prefer the most conservative interpretation that still moves the bug class structurally. If something is contradictory, flag it and ask before proceeding.

        Current plan (markdown):
        # Implementation Plan: Cross-Boundary Contract Guardrails — Revised

## Overview

Same goal as v1: ship runtime guardrails so cross-boundary contract drift becomes structurally loud. Three repos, three branches, three PRs.

**Meta-decision from the critique cluster (FLAG-004 / correctness-4 / scope-1):** porting all selector maps (DIRECT_ROUTE_ALIASES, SPRINT_2_SELECTOR_MAP, SECTION3A_ROUTE_SUPPORT_MAP, etc., ~50 entries + slug encoding) to plpgsql is bigger than this sprint should swallow. The plan is **not** targeting the wrong code, but Layer 2's *scope shape* was wrong. Reframe:
- **`derive_route_key(task_type, params) returns text`** is a small plpgsql function that does the route-key string derivation by consulting either (a) NEW seeded DB tables `route_alias_map(alias, route_key)` and `route_family_for_model(model_name, family)` populated in the same migration from the TS constants, or (b) a lookup join against the existing `route_backend_capabilities` / `route_backend_selectors` tables where the data already lives. Pick (b) where the data already exists; seed (a) only for the residual maps without a DB home.
- **`build_route_contract(task_type, params) returns jsonb`** stays a *TS-side* function in `routeContract.ts`, but rewritten to call `derive_route_key` via RPC for the key portion and to read selector metadata via direct `from('route_backend_selectors').select(...)` queries against the same DB tables. The shape contract is pinned with an explicit TS interface (`RouteContractJSON`) AND a json-schema check in a future hardening sprint (noted as follow-up, not in scope). This satisfies brief Done Criterion #2 (single derive_route_key impl in Postgres; TS calls it via RPC) without requiring a full plpgsql port of the assembler.
- **Worker** stops deriving entirely; reads `params.route_contract` stamped by the edge function.
- **live-test matrix.py** calls the `build_route_contract` RPC by HTTP (PostgREST endpoint or invoking the edge function), eliminating divergence (FLAG-006 / scope-3).

**Settled decisions baked into v2:**
- **model_family collision (FLAG-003 / issue_hints-1):** ONE mechanism only — **worker-internal rename to `model_family_class`**. DB derivation does NOT add a separate ignore-rule. (Drops belt+suspenders per brief mandate.)
- **Sentinel:** Supabase scheduled edge function via `pg_cron` (per SD-001). pg_cron is already in use in this DB (issue_hints-2 — schedules exist in migrations 20250115110000, 20260213200000, etc.), so this is *brownfield extension*, not greenfield infra.
- **Branches:** fresh `fix/contract-enforcement-{db,worker,orchestrator}` off `origin/main`. Explicit verify-upstream-target gate before every `git push` (issue_hints-3).
- **Trigger RPC signature (FLAG-001 / correctness-1):** call `route_backend_claim_decision(p_selector_namespace, p_route_key, p_worker_backend, p_now)` — verbatim from `reigh-app-cloud-chain/supabase/migrations/20260506110000_add_route_backend_selector_control_plane.sql:179-184`.
- **worker_backend at trigger time (FLAG-005 / correctness-2):** use `NEW.selected_backend` if set; else OR-check both `'wgp'` and `'vibecomfy'` — accept if ANY backend returns eligible (matches the permissive semantics of the current claim RPC; rejects only categorically-unclaimable tasks).
- **Cloud-chain worktree (scope-2):** Pre-Phase-A audit confirms which branch carries the *deployed* migrations. If `reigh-app-cloud-chain` (a worktree at a different branch) holds migrations 20260506113000, 20260507165000, 20260507215500 that aren't on `reigh-app/main`, the fix branch MUST be cut from that worktree's HEAD, not from `reigh-app/main`. Step 1 hard-gates on this.
- **Migration timestamps (all_locations-4):** explicit pinned timestamps `20260513120000` (derive_route_key + seed), `20260513120100` (capability inserts + cleanup), `20260513120200` (trigger), `20260513120300` (sentinel infra). Hand-written, not `date +%s`.
- **startup_grace_period_sec (correctness-6):** bump default from 600s to 1200s in the same diff. Cold-start RunPod pods can legitimately exceed 600s; 1200s preserves the safety net without flapping.

## Phase A — reigh-app (DB + edge function)

Branch source TBD by Step 1.

### Step 1: Cloud-chain worktree reconciliation (`reigh-app/`, `reigh-app-cloud-chain/`)
**Scope:** Small — hard gate

These STAY in TS and continue to read the same TS constants — only `deriveRouteKey` / `routeModelFamily` / `routeGuidanceKey` / `routeContinuityCase` / `routeProfile` get reduced (scope-1).

### Step 3: Migration 20260513120000 — `derive_route_key` + seed (`reigh-app/supabase/migrations/`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. **Filename**: `20260513120000_derive_route_key.sql` (explicit timestamp, not placeholder).
2. **Create** lookup tables for the residual TS maps that don't already have a DB home:
   - `route_alias_map(alias text PRIMARY KEY, route_key text NOT NULL)` — seeded from `DIRECT_ROUTE_ALIASES`.
   - `model_family_for_model(model_name text PRIMARY KEY, route_family text NOT NULL CHECK (route_family IN ('wan22_i2v','wan22_vace','ltx2','ltx2_distilled','qwen','z_image')))` — seeded from the model→family mapping currently encoded in `routeModelFamily` (`selectedRoute.ts:445`).
   - Skip `SPRINT_2_SELECTOR_MAP` / `SECTION3A_ROUTE_SUPPORT_MAP` if their data is already in `route_backend_selectors`. Confirm in Step 2.2; if missing, seed an additional `route_support_meta` table.
3. **Create** `public.derive_route_key(p_task_type text, p_params jsonb) returns text LANGUAGE plpgsql STABLE` that:
   - Checks `route_alias_map` for direct alias by `task_type`.
   - For travel/orchestrator types: looks up `model_family_for_model` by `params->>'model_name'`. **Does NOT honor `params->>'model_family'` as an override** — the worker rename eliminates the collision at the write site, so the DB doesn't need a second mechanism (FLAG-003 / issue_hints-1 resolution).
   - Returns NULL if no route key can be derived (caller decides whether NULL is fatal).
4. **In-migration assertions** (`DO $$ ... $$`): hand-table of known cases. Assert `derive_route_key('travel_segment', '{"model_name":"wan22_i2v_a14b"}'::jsonb) = 'model-wan22_i2v'`, plus cases for `wan22_vace`, `ltx2`, `ltx2_distilled`, `qwen`, `z_image`, and orchestrator-parent (`isOrchestratedParentRouteKey` equivalent).
5. **Reversibility**: trailing `-- ROLLBACK:` comment block with `DROP FUNCTION` + `DROP TABLE` (additive migration, reversible).

### Step 4: Migration 20260513120100 — data triage + missing capabilities (`reigh-app/supabase/migrations/`)
**Scope:** Small

2. **[REDACTED]'s chain**: `UPDATE tasks SET status='Failed', last_error='Poisoned parent_route_key mismatch (terminal, do not revive)' WHERE id IN ('0168dcc3-...', 'd2114387-...', '79308f35-...', '47dc41df-...', '2b40a495-...', 'a505b34e-...', 'cc83949d-...') AND status != 'Failed';` (use real column name found in Step 2.2 — `last_error` or `error_reason`).
3. **Other ~40 stuck rows**: `UPDATE tasks SET [REDACTED](task_type, params) WHERE status='Queued' AND route_key IS NULL AND public.derive_route_key(task_type, params) IS NOT NULL;` then `UPDATE tasks SET status='Failed', last_error='Cleanup: unresolvable route_key pre-Layer 1 trigger' WHERE status='Queued' AND route_key IS NULL AND created_at < now() - interval '24 hours';`.
4. **Capability inserts**: `INSERT INTO route_backend_capabilities (...) VALUES ...` for `qwen_image_style` and `animate_character`. Column shape confirmed in Step 2.2; if mapping is unclear, **fence those types out of the trigger** (Step 5.3) and file follow-up in PR.

### Step 5: Migration 20260513120200 — Layer 1 trigger (`reigh-app/supabase/migrations/`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. **Filename**: `20260513120200_tasks_claimable_trigger.sql`.
2. **Define** `public.tasks_assert_claimable() returns trigger LANGUAGE plpgsql AS $$`:
   ```
   DECLARE v_namespace text; v_backend text; v_decision record; v_eligible boolean := false;
   BEGIN
     IF NEW.params->'route_contract' IS NULL OR NEW.params->'route_contract' = 'null'::jsonb THEN
       RAISE EXCEPTION 'route_contract missing in params for Queued task (route_key=%, task_type=%)', NEW.route_key, NEW.task_type USING ERRCODE = 'check_violation';
     END IF;
     v_namespace := COALESCE(NEW.selector_namespace, 'production');
     -- Per FLAG-005: if selected_backend is set, check just that one; otherwise OR-check wgp + vibecomfy
     IF NEW.selected_backend IS NOT NULL THEN
       SELECT * INTO v_decision FROM public.route_backend_claim_decision(v_namespace, NEW.route_key, NEW.selected_backend, now());
       v_eligible := v_decision.eligible;
     ELSE
       FOREACH v_backend IN ARRAY ARRAY['wgp','vibecomfy'] LOOP
         SELECT * INTO v_decision FROM public.route_backend_claim_decision(v_namespace, NEW.route_key, v_backend, now());
         IF v_decision.eligible THEN v_eligible := true; EXIT; END IF;
       END LOOP;
     END IF;
     IF NOT v_eligible THEN
       RAISE EXCEPTION 'route_contract validation failed: % (route_key=%, task_type=%, namespace=%)', v_decision.reason, NEW.route_key, NEW.task_type, v_namespace USING ERRCODE = 'check_violation';
     END IF;
     RETURN NEW;
   END; $$;
   ```
   Signature taken verbatim from `20260506110000:179-184` (FLAG-001 / correctness-1).
3. **Attach**: `CREATE TRIGGER tasks_assert_claimable BEFORE INSERT OR UPDATE OF status, route_key, params, selected_backend, selector_namespace ON tasks FOR EACH ROW WHEN (NEW.status = 'Queued' AND NEW.task_type NOT LIKE '%_orchestrator') EXECUTE FUNCTION public.tasks_assert_claimable();`
4. **In-migration assertions**: insert bad row → expect raise; insert orchestrator type with null route_key → expect success; insert valid row with route_contract → expect success.
5. **Note in migration comment** (scope-4): trigger is STRICTER than current `claim_next_task_service_role` semantics (which only inline-validates `t.params->'route_contract'`). This is intentional — early gate prevents the categorically-unclaimable creation that bug #2 is about. Reanimate paths (`status` flipped back to `'Queued'` on a legacy row) WILL fire the trigger; the RAISE message names the violated invariant so admins see the cause (all_locations-3).

**Scope:** Medium

If any sync caller cannot be migrated (test fixture with sync setup), leave it on a sync helper that uses a hardcoded fixture map AND assert the fixture map matches the DB via a CI-style test (deferred — note in PR).

### Step 7: Migration 20260513120300 — Layer 4 sentinel infra (`reigh-app/supabase/migrations/`, `reigh-app/supabase/functions/`)
**Scope:** Medium

---

## Phase B — reigh-worker (Layer 0 rename + Layer 2 retirement)

Branch: `fix/contract-enforcement-worker` off `origin/main` of `reigh-worker`. Wait for Phase A deploy.

### Step 9: Rename worker-internal field to `model_family_class` — NARROWED SCOPE (`reigh-worker/source/task_handlers/`)
**Scope:** Small — narrowed per FLAG-002 / correctness-3
1. **Real worker-internal {ltx,wan} sites** to rename — only these:
   - `tasks/task_registry.py:113-117` (TravelSegmentPayload.__post_init__)
   - `tasks/task_registry.py:300-302` (_get_param model_family check)
   - `tasks/task_registry.py:1157-1163` (SVI gating)
   - `travel/orchestrator.py:436-437` — the WRITE site. **Decision (callers-3)**: write `model_family_class = <worker-internal value>` (NOT drop the write). `task_registry.py:300` still needs to read it for FPS/frame-step decisions; renaming the key, not dropping the data.
   - `_derive_model_family` helper itself (rename return-target key in its callers).
2. **DO NOT rename** `scripts/live_test/matrix.py:197, 205, 285, 298, 436, 463, 538, 561, 820, 837, 1012` — those write route-namespace values (`wan22_i2v`, `wan22_vace`, etc.) that intentionally drive `routeModelFamily()`'s override. They stay as `model_family`. Documented in PR.
3. **Grep verification**: after rename, `rg "params\[['\"]model_family['\"]\]" source/` should show only route-namespace reads (or none); `rg "model_family_class" source/` shows the renamed reads.

### Step 10: Retire local route derivation (`reigh-worker/source/task_handlers/tasks/template_routing.py`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. **Replace** `_route_model_family` (989), `_route_guidance_kind` (1005), `_route_guidance_mode` (1012), `_route_guidance_key` (1019), `_route_continuity_case` (~1032) with thin readers of `task['params']['route_contract']` (DB-stamped). Worker no longer derives.
2. **Keep** `validate_existing_child_route_contracts` and all other VALIDATION helpers unchanged.

### Step 11: Make live-test matrix.py call `build_route_contract` via RPC — explicit decision (scope-3 / FLAG-006) (`reigh-worker/scripts/live_test/matrix.py`)
**Scope:** Small — explicit commitment, no longer deferred
1. **Decision**: `matrix.py:_route_contract` (line 627) STOPS building the contract locally and calls the deployed Supabase edge function `create-task` (which now uses RPC under the hood), OR — if matrix.py writes directly to tasks (bypassing edge function) — calls `supabase.rpc('derive_route_key', ...)` via HTTP to PostgREST. Check which path matrix.py uses; pick the matching call.
2. **Dependency check**: matrix.py already imports `requests` / `httpx`? If yes, use that for the PostgREST call. If no — and no supabase-py — fall back to a minimal `urllib.request.Request` POST. NO new deps (brief constraint).
3. **Parity test**: add `tests/live_test/test_route_contract_parity.py` that calls the RPC for the 6 enum cases and asserts the returned route_key matches a hand-table — guards against future drift.

### Step 12: Push reigh-worker branch — verify upstream gate
**Scope:** Small
1. **Pre-push gate (issue_hints-3)**: `git rev-parse --abbrev-ref --symbolic-full-name @{u}` MUST be `origin/fix/contract-enforcement-worker`, NOT any `megaplan/vibecomfy-sprint-09-*` branch. Abort push if mismatch.
2. **Push**. PR description: lists Layer 0 rename (narrowed) + Layer 2 retirement + live-test parity. Notes pods must be cycled after merge.

---

## Phase C — reigh-worker-orchestrator (Layer 3 + sentinel reader)

Branch: `fix/contract-enforcement-orchestrator` off `origin/main`.

### Step 13: Layer 3 conv #1 — drop queue gate + bump grace period (`gpu_orchestrator/worker_state.py`, `gpu_orchestrator/config.py`)
**Scope:** Small
1. **Edit** `worker_state.py:271-276`: remove `queued_count > 0` from the STARTUP_NEVER_READY condition. Final: `if not has_ever_claimed and effective_age_sec > config.startup_grace_period_sec: terminate('STARTUP_NEVER_READY')`.
2. **Bump (correctness-6)**: `config.py:167` — `startup_grace_period_sec` default 600 → **1200**. Cold-start RunPod pods on slow images legitimately exceed 600s; 1200s keeps the gate active without flapping.

### Step 14: Layer 3 conv #2 — periodic failsafe exception (`gpu_orchestrator/control/phases/periodic.py`)
**Scope:** Small
1. **Read** `WorkerState` to confirm `lifecycle` attribute and an age field. Add `WorkerState.age_in_lifecycle_sec` accessor if missing.
2. **Edit** line 214: insert the ACTIVE_INITIALIZING-past-N-seconds exception BEFORE the existing `continue`. Threshold `config.active_initializing_max_sec` default 1800.

### Step 15: Layer 3 conv #3 — launcher preflight kill gate
**Scope:** Small — search-then-patch
1. **Locate**: `rg -n 'preflight|PREFLIGHT|Timed out waiting' reigh-worker/ reigh-worker-orchestrator/scripts/`. Inspect `worker_startup.template.sh` plus any `.py` it invokes.
2. **If found**: append `sys.exit(1)` / `exit 1` directly after the log. **If not found**: explicit note in PR — Done Criterion #4 marked "unable to satisfy as worded; brief admits string missing" and ask user for the correct line.

### Step 16: Sentinel pause_scaling reader (`gpu_orchestrator/control/phases/periodic.py`)
**Scope:** Small
1. Add a single read of `pause_scaling` table at the start of each scaling-up tick. If `pool` row exists and `until > now()`, skip scale-up for that pool. No state-machine surgery.

### Step 17: Commit debug-CLI scripts (`reigh-worker-orchestrator/scripts/debug/`)
**Scope:** Small
1. `git status` — verify whether `worker.py` / `workers.py` / `deployment-drift` are actually uncommitted. If clean: skip. If dirty: commit on this branch.

### Step 18: Push reigh-worker-orchestrator branch — verify upstream gate
**Scope:** Small
1. **Pre-push gate (issue_hints-3)**: upstream MUST be `origin/fix/contract-enforcement-orchestrator`, NOT `megaplan/vibecomfy-sprint-07-orchestrator-pools-artifacts` (Railway auto-deploys from that). Abort push if mismatch.
2. **Push**. PR description: Layer 3 + sentinel reader; no Railway auto-deploy because new branch is not the tracked branch.

---

## Phase D — Verification

### Step 19: Targeted tests per repo
**Scope:** Small
1. reigh-app: `deno test` on routeContract + claim-next-task; psql probes for trigger.
2. reigh-worker-orchestrator: `PYENV_VERSION=3.11.11 pytest tests/gpu_orchestrator/` + new tests for Layer 3 conversions.
3. reigh-worker: existing tests touched by the rename; new parity test from Step 11.3.

### Step 20: End-to-end probes
**Scope:** Small
1. Fresh `travel_orchestrator` task via deployed `create-task` → expect claim → fanout → segments → parent Complete.
2. One pass of `reigh-worker/scripts/live_test/` post-deploy.
3. Sentinel page test: synthesize a stuck-Queued state (admin-side INSERT bypassing trigger via service role on a fenced orchestrator type), confirm webhook fires within 5-6 min, clean up.
4. Direct-SQL re-probes for trigger.

### Step 21: Open 3 PRs (not merged)
**Scope:** Small
1. Each PR description: layer(s) implemented + link to brief + explicit "DO NOT merge to sprint-07 / sprint-09" line.

---

## Execution Order

1. **Phase A** end-to-end (Step 1 cloud-chain gate is hard prerequisite). Migrations land in alphabetical = timestamp order: `120000` → `120100` → `120200` → `120300`.
2. **Phase B** and **Phase C** parallel after Phase A deploys.
3. **Phase D** last.

## Validation Order

1. Migration-internal `DO $$` asserts (cheapest, during `db push`).
2. Deno tests for `routeContract.test.ts` + `claim-next-task/index.test.ts`.
3. Direct-SQL trigger probes (good + bad inserts).
4. Orchestrator unit tests for Layer 3 conversions.
5. Worker live-test parity test (Step 11.3).
6. End-to-end `travel_orchestrator` task on deployed prod.
7. Live-test pass.
8. Sentinel page test.

    "OR-loop error message bug (correctness-1) \u2014 when both backends fail, accumulate reasons across the loop and RAISE with both. Pseudocode: `v_reasons text[] := ARRAY[]::text[]; FOREACH ... ELSE v_reasons := array_append(v_reasons, format('%s: %s', v_backend, v_decision.reason)); END IF; END LOOP; IF NOT v_eligible THEN RAISE ... using array_to_string(v_reasons, '; '); END IF;`",
    "startup_grace_period_sec env override (correctness-2) \u2014 Step 13.2 must also include: 'after PR merges, verify Railway env var STARTUP_GRACE_PERIOD_SEC is unset or set to >=1200. If set lower, bump it via Railway UI before deploying.' Otherwise the code default change is a no-op.",
